- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) leave the generic message — the `codex doctor` hint actively misleads for an account-type restriction; (b) detect the account type upfront and refuse to attempt an incompatible pinned fallback — extra `codex doctor`/auth probe + coupling for a deliberate-override edge case, where a clear post-failure message is sufficient; (c) widen `QUOTA_SIGNALS` to absorb the 400 so it routes through the normal exhaustion path — wrong classification (a 400 account restriction is not quota) and would corrupt `isQuotaError`'s contract, which other call sites depend on.
- **Verification:** TDD (RED → GREEN): the predicate test and the executor message test both failed first for the right reason (missing function; generic message lacking `ASK_CODEX_FALLBACK_MODEL`), then passed after the minimal implementation. `ask-codex-mcp` 98 tests + lint (`biome` + `tsc --noEmit`) clean; full monorepo suite green.
- **Review refinement (PR #198, claude-review finding 1):** the actionable message originally hard-coded "gpt-5.4-mini (the default)… or unset it to use the default", but the `isModelUnavailableError` branch fires for *any* fallback 400 — including a hypothetical where the default `gpt-5.4-mini` itself is rejected — in which case recommending it back is self-contradictory. Fixed by conditioning the remediation sentence on `process.env.ASK_CODEX_FALLBACK_MODEL` (the same var `MODELS.FALLBACK` resolves from, so its presence distinguishes a user pin from the built-in default): pinned → "set a supported model, or unset to use the default `gpt-5.4-mini`"; unpinned → "set `ASK_CODEX_FALLBACK_MODEL` to a supported model" (no contradictory self-reference). +2 tests (`ask-codex-mcp` 98 green). Finding 2 (sibling-branch drift) was a no-action maintainability nit.
- **Reference:** `packages/codex-mcp/src/constants.ts` (`MODEL_UNAVAILABLE_SIGNALS`), `packages/codex-mcp/src/utils/codexExecutor.ts` (`isModelUnavailableError`, fallback `catch`), tests `quota-detection.test.ts` + `codexExecutor.test.ts`; ADR-028 (single-step fallback), ADR-123 (codex-pair structural-unavailability), ADR-126 (default fallback `gpt-5.4-mini`); issue #196 (closes), PR #195 (origin of the follow-up).

## ADR-126: Codex quota-fallback model `gpt-5.5-mini` → `gpt-5.4-mini` (supersedes ADR-067's fallback choice; refines ADR-123; closes #194)

- **Date:** 2026-06-20
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on `ask-codex-mcp` (`constants.ts` `MODELS.FALLBACK`) and `@ask-llm/plugin` (`codex-pair-defaults.json` + `codex-pair-watch.mjs` inline default). The `gpt-5.5` primary default is unchanged. `ask-codex-mcp` 90 tests + `@ask-llm/plugin` 415 tests green — including the JSON↔`constants.ts` drift guard and the codex-pair structural-unavailability test. Changeset `codex-fallback-gpt-5.4-mini` (patch `ask-codex-mcp`, patch `@ask-llm/plugin`; `ask-llm-mcp` auto-bumps as a dependent).
- **Context:** ADR-067 set the Codex quota fallback to `gpt-5.5-mini`. The 2026-06-19 drift-audit (#194) flagged it as a non-existent model and recommended `gpt-5.4-mini`. The "model doesn't exist" framing was imprecise — a live probe on this machine's ChatGPT-plan codex (`codex exec -m <model> "ping"`, CLI 0.141.0) returned, for `gpt-5.5-mini`, **`400 "The 'gpt-5.5-mini' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account."`** (an account-type restriction, not a 404), while `gpt-5.4-mini` answered `pong` (exit 0). This is the same 400 ADR-123 discovered. ADR-123 deliberately **kept** `gpt-5.5-mini` (alternative (b) rejected: "valid for API-key users… the failure is account-type-specific") and instead handled the ChatGPT-plan 400 with a clean codex-pair auto-pause. The gap ADR-123 left: a clean *pause* is not a working *fallback* — on ChatGPT-plan accounts, which are the **common case** for the `codex` CLI (plan quota is account-wide), the quota fallback never produced the intended cheaper answer.
- **Decision:** Change `MODELS.FALLBACK` default from `gpt-5.5-mini` to `gpt-5.4-mini`. `gpt-5.4-mini` is confirmed to work on ChatGPT-plan accounts (the common case) and is a valid cheaper model for API-key accounts, so it is the safer universal default. Mirror the value in `codex-pair-defaults.json` and the `codex-pair-watch.mjs` inline literal (the drift-guard test fails CI if these diverge from `constants.ts`). The ADR-123 structural-unavailability handling in codex-pair is **retained** — it is now a defensive guard for users who *pin* an unavailable model via `ASK_CODEX_FALLBACK_MODEL` (e.g. an API-key user who pins `gpt-5.5-mini` and later changes account type). API-key users who prefer `gpt-5.5-mini` can pin it via the env var; the primary `gpt-5.5` default is unchanged.
- **Relationship to prior ADRs:** Supersedes the *fallback-model* sub-decision of **ADR-067** (which bumped fallback `gpt-5.4-mini` → `gpt-5.5-mini`); the default-model bump to `gpt-5.5` from ADR-067 stands. Refines **ADR-123**: that ADR's reading of the cause (account-type-specific, not non-existent) was correct, but its choice to keep `gpt-5.5-mini` as the default under-served the common ChatGPT-plan case; the live evidence + common-case framing reverses ADR-123's rejected alternative (b). The single-step fallback contract from **ADR-028** is unchanged — this is a one-constant value change, not an executor change.
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) keep `gpt-5.5-mini` and only improve the published codex-mcp error message (port ADR-123's `isModelUnavailableError` clean-exhaustion message into `codexExecutor.ts`) — graceful, but still leaves the common ChatGPT-plan case with no working cheaper fallback; (b) chained fallback `gpt-5.5-mini → gpt-5.4-mini` — would depart from ADR-028's single-step swap contract (iterate a model array) for marginal gain over simply defaulting to the model that works everywhere; (c) detect the account type upfront via `codex doctor` auth-mode and pick the fallback accordingly — extra probe + coupling; the universal-default approach is simpler and overridable.
- **Verification:** Live two-model probe (above). Full `ask-codex-mcp` + `@ask-llm/plugin` suites green; drift guard confirms `constants.ts` ↔ `codex-pair-defaults.json` agreement.
- **Reference:** `packages/codex-mcp/src/constants.ts`, `packages/claude-plugin/codex-pair-defaults.json`, `packages/claude-plugin/scripts/codex-pair-watch.mjs`, tests `smoke.test.ts` + `codex-pair-watch.test.ts` (drift guard, structural-unavailability); ADR-028 (single-step fallback), ADR-067 (gpt-5.5 bump), ADR-123 (codex-pair structural-unavailability handling); issue #194.

## ADR-125: Antigravity default → Gemini 3.1 Pro (High) with a Gemini 3.5 Flash (High) rate-limit fallback

- **Date:** 2026-06-18
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on `ask-antigravity-mcp` (`constants.ts` `MODELS.DEFAULT`/`MODELS.FALLBACK`; `antigravityExecutor.ts` `runWithModel` extraction + retry; tool description; llm-mcp registry `defaultModel`; brainstorm-coordinator pin; package/docs/root README). Unit suite 35 green (4 new fallback tests), `ask-antigravity-mcp` build + `ask-llm-mcp` typecheck clean. Verified live against `agy` 1.0.6: the local executor invoked `agy --model "Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)"`, exit 0 in 13.9s, structured `model` reported the Pro tier. Changeset `antigravity-default-pro-flash-fallback` (minor `ask-antigravity-mcp`, patch `ask-llm-mcp` + `@ask-llm/plugin`). **Review follow-up (PR #192, Codex P2):** the unified `ask-llm`/`multi-llm` layer read only `usage?.model ?? defaultModel` and ignored the executor's top-level `result.model`, so a fallback answer was mislabeled as Pro for unified users — a latent gap this change activated (default and `result.model` previously coincided). Fixed by adding `model?` to the `ExecutorFn` return contract and threading `result.model` through both attribution sites (`index.ts:193`, `multiLlm.ts:83`); +2 llm-mcp tests (58 green).
- **Context:** `ask-antigravity` shipped (ADR-116, PR #167) defaulting to **Gemini 3.5 Flash (High)** with **no fallback** at all — the executor resolved exactly one model (`options.model` → `ASK_ANTIGRAVITY_MODEL` → `MODELS.DEFAULT`) and a rate limit just threw the actionable `RATE_LIMITED` message. For the maintainer's core workload (second opinion, plan critique, diff review) the stronger reasoning tier is the better default, and `agy models` exposes `Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)` as a verbatim `--model` string that works under `-p`. Leading with Pro raises the odds of hitting a Google AI Pro/Ultra subscription rate limit, so a graceful degradation path is wanted rather than a hard failure. `ask-gemini` (Pro→Flash) and `ask-codex` (gpt-5.5→gpt-5.5-mini) already establish the cross-tier quota-fallback pattern in this repo.
- **Decision:** Default `ask-antigravity` to **Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)** and add **Gemini 3.5 Flash (High)** as a rate-limit-only fallback. Mechanically: extract a `runWithModel(model)` closure (shared prompt/timeout/sandbox) so each attempt stamps its **own `startedAt`** — the transcript scraper (`readLatestResponse`) reads files written *after* that timestamp, so a reused timestamp would risk the fallback reading the rate-limited primary's (absent) transcript. The whole try/fallback stays **inside `withMutex`**, preserving the correctness lock that no two `agy -p` runs race on the shared `last_conversations.json`. Fallback fires **only** on `isRateLimitError` (RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED / 429 / quota / "rate limit" / "too many requests"); when the primary is rate-limited and `primaryModel !== MODELS.FALLBACK`, retry once on Flash. If the resolved model already *is* the fallback (default flipped, or caller pinned it), there is nothing to fall back to → `RATE_LIMITED`. A non-rate-limit fallback failure (timeout, crash) is surfaced as-is, never masked as `RATE_LIMITED`; non-rate-limit primary errors (auth, not-installed, `NO_OUTPUT`) skip the fallback entirely because a different model fails identically. The raw `agy` call in `brainstorm-coordinator` pins the new default but **does not** inherit the fallback (it bypasses the executor) — documented inline.
- **Relationship to ADR (Ollama no-fallback, #191):** This is intentionally the *opposite* direction from the recent Ollama change, and not a contradiction. Ollama **removed** a *silent same-family substitution* (qwen3.6:27b → 1.5b) that masked a missing-model misconfiguration and degraded answer quality without telling anyone. Antigravity **adds** a *cross-tier quota fallback* where the primary is genuinely throttled and a working tier still exists — the model that produced the answer is reported in the structured `AskResponse.model`, so the substitution is observable, not silent. Different failure mode → different policy.
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) keep Flash as default — under-powers the reasoning-heavy second-opinion workload the tool is built for; (b) Pro default with no fallback — a single subscription rate limit becomes a hard failure when a usable cheaper tier exists; (c) fall back on *any* error — auth / not-installed / timeout fail the same on Flash, so retrying wastes a turn and can mask the real cause; restrict to the rate-limit signal; (d) surface `fellBack` in a `usage` object like `ask-gemini` — `agy` emits no token stats (usage stays `undefined`); the actual-model-used signal rides in `AskResponse.model` instead — though this *did* require threading the executor's `result.model` through the unified layer (`ExecutorFn` previously hid it, so unified callers fell through to `defaultModel`; fixed per the review follow-up above) rather than adding a parallel `fellBack` flag; (e) give the brainstorm raw-`agy` path its own fallback — it deliberately bypasses the executor for a single foreground dispatch (ADR-050), so a second invocation there would complicate the `wait`-based join for little gain.
- **Reference:** `packages/antigravity-mcp/src/constants.ts`, `packages/antigravity-mcp/src/utils/antigravityExecutor.ts`, tests `antigravityExecutor.test.ts` ("rate-limit fallback" suite); ADR-116 (model selection), ADR-050 (brainstorm single-dispatch).

## ADR-124: Documentation reposition — second-opinion-first, provider-neutral README + docs site

- **Date:** 2026-06-15
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on `docs/readme-uiux-rewrite` (11-task plan, one commit per task; every task left `yarn docs:build` green; internal-link audit clean; `/multi-review` run on the full diff before merge). Docs-only — no package/test/changeset impact (the root README and `apps/docs/` are not published packages; the site deploys via `deploy-docs.yml`).
- **Context:** The README and VitePress site led with the MCP *mechanism* ("AI-to-AI collaboration via MCP") and put **Gemini first** on every surface (intro, hero, nav, sidebar, provider cards). But Gemini CLI is enterprise-gated as of 2026-06-18 (the `gemini.md` tier banner), so the most-prominent provider is the one most readers can no longer use. Antigravity — the Google-sanctioned successor and a first-class orchestrator provider (`PROVIDERS.antigravity` in llm-mcp) — had **no homepage card** and was missing from several provider lists. A line-by-line pass also surfaced prose that had drifted from code: `how-to-ask` listed the `ask-llm` `provider` enum as gemini/codex/ollama (omitting antigravity); `agents.md` documented only 3 of 4 reviewers (no `antigravity-reviewer`); `models.md`/FAQ still advertised a Gemini "free tier via OAuth"; `how-it-works`/FAQ said "three providers". Accessibility: `--color-text-muted` (#52525b on #0a0a0b) was ~2.5:1, below WCAG AA.
- **Decision:** Reposition all surfaces to **lead with the value (a second opinion before you ship), not the protocol**, and present providers **by use-case fit, Codex/Antigravity-forward**, with Gemini documented but flagged enterprise-gated. Canonical provider order everywhere: Codex → Antigravity → Ollama → Gemini → Unified. Concrete changes: README + homepage rewritten to the arc Hook → What → In-action → Three jobs → Pick a reviewer → Install; a new `InAction.vue` transcript component and a homepage Antigravity card (+ an "enterprise" tag on Gemini); the two sidebar-orphaned pages (`installation`, `first-steps`) de-orphaned into a Quick Start → Installation → First Steps progression with distinct, de-duplicated roles (verify now lives canonically in First Steps); per-provider "Best for / Not for" framing; `strategies-and-examples` reframed as task recipes; and a WCAG-AA contrast fix (`--color-text-muted` → #82828c, ~5.2:1). The code-drift findings above were corrected as part of the pass. **Refine, not redesign** — the dark/mono VitePress theme, routes, and reference tables are preserved (no light mode, no route renames, no SEO breakage).
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) keep Gemini-forward with warnings — front-loads a provider most users can't use; (b) major IA overhaul / new taxonomy — risks broken links and discards polished structure for no decided benefit; (c) delete the orphan pages — `installation.md` is linked from `faq.md` and both held unique content, so de-orphaning with distinct roles beats deletion; (d) a duplicate Gemini banner on `gemini.md` (as the plan first drafted) — the page already had a stronger `::: danger` + tier section, so only the Best-for/Not-for framing was added.
- **Reference:** spec [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-14-docs-readme-uiux-rewrite-design.md`](superpowers/specs/2026-06-14-docs-readme-uiux-rewrite-design.md); plan [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-14-docs-readme-uiux-rewrite.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-06-14-docs-readme-uiux-rewrite.md).

## ADR-123: codex-pair — a structurally-unavailable fallback model after a primary quota is exhaustion (#176 follow-up)

- **Date:** 2026-06-14
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on `fix/codex-pair-chatgpt-fallback` (TDD: fake-codex `quota-plan-chatgpt` scenario + test; full plugin suite 415 green; lint clean). Surfaced by dogfooding — the ADR-120 backstop auto-paused mid-session.
- **Context:** ADR-120's `runCodexWithFallback` falls back `gpt-5.5 → gpt-5.5-mini` only after a **primary quota** error, then does the clean #176 quota auto-pause (with reset hint) when the *fallback* also hits quota (`:874`) or when there is no ladder at all (`model === fallbackModel`, `:880`). It missed a third path: the fallback model being **structurally unavailable**. On ChatGPT-plan Codex accounts `gpt-5.5-mini` is rejected with a `400 "is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account"` — *not* a quota error — so the fallback error went untagged and the real primary-quota exhaustion was lost to the 3-failure backstop (`kind:"failures"`, no reset hint). Root insight: control flow proves exhaustion — we only reach the fallback catch because the primary was quota-dead, so if the fallback also can't run (own quota OR structurally unavailable) there is no usable model. On ChatGPT plans this is the *common* case, since plan quota is account-wide and a cheaper fallback never applied.
- **Decision:** Add `isModelUnavailableError` (signal `"is not supported when using codex with a chatgpt"`). In the fallback-failed catch, when the fallback error matches it, treat the situation as the same "no usable model" exhaustion as the no-ladder case: tag `quotaExhausted` and **re-throw the primary quota error** (not the fallback 400) so its reason + reset hint reach the clean #176 quota auto-pause. Fallback-also-quota keeps its existing behavior (tag + throw the fallback error). The published `ask-codex-mcp` executor shares the same fallback assumption but already throws a clear terminal error (no cascade) — left unchanged to keep this fix focused.
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) proactively detect ChatGPT-account upfront via `codex doctor` auth-mode and skip the mini fallback — extra probe + coupling; the reactive signal-on-fallback is simpler and localized; (b) change/remove the default fallback model — `gpt-5.5-mini` is valid for API-key users, so removing it would regress them; the failure is account-type-specific; (c) throw the fallback 400 tagged `quotaExhausted` — the pause notice would show an opaque model-support error and lose the primary's reset hint; (d) treat ANY fallback failure as exhaustion — a transient fallback error shouldn't pause; restrict to the structural model-unavailable signal.
- **Reference:** `packages/claude-plugin/scripts/codex-pair-watch.mjs` (`runCodexWithFallback`, `isModelUnavailableError`); test `codex-pair-watch.test.ts` "primary quota + fallback model unavailable (ChatGPT plan)"; `docs/BUGS.md`.

## ADR-122: `ask-llm doctor` codex-diagnostics enrichment via a generic `enrich` hook (#183)

- **Date:** 2026-06-14
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on `feat/183-codex-doctor-enrichment` (shared `enrich` hook + render, codex parser/runner, llm-mcp wiring; +3 shared doctor tests, +8 codex doctor tests, all green; verified end-to-end against codex 0.139 — text + `--json`).
- **Context:** #183 (the "I1" thread carried across five upstream-sync audits, #131 → #152) asked to fold codex's machine-readable `codex doctor --json` health into `ask-llm doctor`, gated to codex ≥0.137, without changing default output. `runDiagnostics` (`packages/shared/src/doctor.ts`) is deliberately provider-agnostic — it probes every provider generically (`which` + `--version`) and already exposes one optional per-provider capability callback (`probeAvailability`, used by HTTP providers like ollama). Hardcoding codex branches into shared would break that boundary. Empirically (`codex doctor --json`, codex 0.139): a structured `{ overallStatus, codexVersion, checks: { <id>: { category, status, summary, remediation } } }` report that maps near 1:1 onto ask-llm's own check model, exits 0 even on `warning`, and reports **local install/auth/config health, not live API/quota state**.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Generic `enrich` hook**, mirroring `probeAvailability`: `ProviderSpec.enrich?(ctx: { command; pathEnv }) => Promise<ProviderEnrichment | undefined>`, run by `runDiagnostics` only when the CLI probes *available*, wrapped in try/catch. `ProviderProbe` gains optional `enrichment`; new `ProviderEnrichment { heading, overall, checks[] }` type. shared never learns codex shells out.
  2. **Capability-probe, not version-gate.** The codex enricher attempts `codex doctor --json` and degrades to `undefined` on failure — **except** that a non-zero exit still carrying a valid JSON report on stdout is salvaged and surfaced (codex emits the full report even when exiting non-zero on error-level health — the ADR-117 stdout-union pattern; flagged HIGH by codex-pair during this build). No `codex --version` parsing — the issue's "≥0.137" enforces itself by the probe succeeding. Rejects the issue's literal version-gate because version strings are brittle (the pre-release/nightly parsing the upstream-sync audits kept tripping on).
  3. **Scope: `codex doctor` only.** `codex plugin list --json` (codex *marketplace* plugins) dropped as YAGNI — unrelated to ask-llm's `codex exec` usage.
  4. **Informational only.** codex status maps onto ask-llm's vocabulary (`ok→pass`, `warning→warn`, `error→fail`) but never affects `report.status` or the doctor exit code; text mode shows overall + non-ok checks only, the full mapped list rides along in `--json`.
  5. **Placement honors the package graph:** pure `parseCodexDoctorJson` + `enrichCodexDoctor` (injectable runner) live in `ask-codex-mcp` (beside `parseCodexJsonlOutput`), re-exported on `ask-codex-mcp/executor`; llm-mcp wires it via new `enrichModule`/`enrichFn` config loaded by the **same variable-specifier dynamic `import()`** as `probeAvailability` (ADR-119-safe; graceful if codex-mcp absent).
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) codex branches in `shared/doctor.ts` — breaks the provider-agnostic boundary; (b) version-gate on `codex --version ≥0.137` — brittle string parsing; (c) static import of the codex enricher in llm-mcp — tsdown could inline it and it crashes when codex-mcp is absent; the config-driven dynamic import degrades gracefully; (d) `codex plugin list` inclusion — YAGNI; (e) rolling codex health into ask-llm's overall status/exit code — false alarms (codex `warning` for "app-server not running" doesn't mean ask-llm is broken).
- **Reference:** `docs/plans/2026-06-14-codex-doctor-enrichment-design.md`; issue #183.

## ADR-121: Consolidate the `kano:indifferent` upstream-sync audit backlog — #151/#152 → #183, close notice #171

- **Date:** 2026-06-14
- **Status:** Accepted — executed (issues closed, #183 filed). Extends ADR-109's audit-cadence retirement.
- **Context:** A Kano pass over the open backlog (2026-06-14) found **zero** `must-be`/`performance`/`attractive` issues — all three open issues were defer/close/consolidate: #151 + #152 (rolling upstream-sync audit records, both `kano:indifferent`/`effort:s`, both self-stating "zero outstanding correctness gaps" and self-offering to close) and #171 (an external CodeGuilds listing notice, no labels, body literally says "feel free to close it"). The `KANO-TRIAGE.md` "Current triage" table was fully stale — every issue it listed (#139–#145) is already closed. The one piece of real code work was buried inside the audit issues as **"I1"**: enrich `ask-llm doctor` with `codex doctor --json` + `codex plugin list --json`. It had been carried verbatim across **five** audits (#131 → #139 I2 → #145 I1 → #151 I1 → #152 I1) without ever being done or explicitly killed — a triage smell (a thread that survives that many passes is either worth doing or worth closing; carrying it a sixth time is the bad outcome).
- **Decision:** Apply the Kano "consolidate" action literally. (1) File **#183** as a single first-class, properly-triaged tracker (`kano:performance`/`effort:s`) capturing the live threads — **I1** (the `ask-llm doctor` enrichment, the only code work; `packages/shared/src/doctor.ts`) + **I2** (a ~5-min manual `codex exec resume` smoke-test against codex ≥0.137) — with explicit design constraints (version-gate on codex ≥0.137; default `--ignore-user-config` doctor output unchanged). (2) Close #151 + #152 as `not_planned`, each pointing to #183. (3) Close #171 as `not_planned` (external notice, not a work item). (4) Reaffirm that upstream-CLI watching is **on-demand, not per-release** (ADR-109): no audit issue per upstream release; re-audit only when a *stable* release observably breaks a flag / JSONL / env-var / model-default surface we depend on. The "watch, don't act" items the audits tracked (codex 0.138-alpha notes-render glitch; gemini-cli 0.46/0.47 stable promotion) are intentionally **not** carried into #183.
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) **Build I1 immediately** — it's gated so default users see no change (low Kano signal), and committing to a sight-unseen implementation pre-empts the maintainer's call; promoting it to a real, schedulable issue is the lighter, reversible move. (b) **Close all three and drop I1 entirely** — discards a thread five audits independently judged worth noting; the ~40-LOC additive enrichment is cheap enough that a clean tracker beats silent abandonment. (c) **Keep carrying the threads inside per-release audit issues** — the exact pattern ADR-109 retired and this smell re-exposes.
- **Reference:** issue #183; `docs/KANO-TRIAGE.md` (triage table refreshed in the same change).

## ADR-120: codex-pair auto-pauses itself on provider failure (#176)

- **Date:** 2026-06-11
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on branch `feat/176-codex-pair-auto-pause` (state helpers, parseResetHint, error classification, quota auto-pause, failure backstop, skill docs; plugin suite green).
- **Context:** Once Codex quota was exhausted, the PostToolUse hook failed on every edit for the rest of the session with an unhelpful reason: "Reading prompt from stdin..." — the stderr banner. The real error is a stdout JSONL `error` event that the non-zero-exit path discarded (ADR-117's `commandExecutor` union fix applied to the shared layer, but the hook's own rejection-reason construction still grabbed stderr first). `QUOTA_SIGNALS` only matched API-style error phrasings; ChatGPT-plan quota messages ("You've hit your usage limit") bypassed even the existing model fallback because typographic vs ASCII apostrophes caused substring mismatches. The plugin had a pause mechanism (`/codex-pair-pause` → `state/paused` sentinel) but the hook never invoked it — quota exhaustion produced an error cascade instead of a single, clean pause with a user-facing notice.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Rejection reason**: on non-zero exit, surface the last stdout JSONL `error` event first, falling back to the stderr tail, then `codex exit <code>` — so the real quota text reaches the quota classifier instead of the benign stdin banner.
  2. **QUOTA_SIGNALS widened**: add `"usage limit"` and `"rate limit"` as apostrophe-free substrings so ChatGPT-plan quota messages match regardless of whether the apostrophe is ASCII (`'`) or typographic (`'`).
  3. **Both-models-quota → auto-pause**: when both gpt-5.5 and gpt-5.5-mini are quota-exhausted, set `err.quotaExhausted = true`; the hook writes the pause sentinel itself and emits a one-time notice that includes the parsed reset hint (extracted by a pure `parseResetHint(text)` from the quota message's "try again at `<date>`" clause — display-only, not acted on).
  4. **Backstop**: 3 consecutive non-quota failures of any kind (global per project, any tool) → same auto-pause. Counter stored in `state/failures.json`; clears on any successful live review. Cache hits do not touch the counter.
  5. **Sentinel reuse**: the same `state/paused` file as `/codex-pair-pause`. Empty body = manual pause; JSON body = auto pause with `kind`/`reason`/`resetHint`/`at` fields. Written with flag `"wx"` (exclusive create) — an existing pause is never clobbered, which also makes notify-once hold under concurrent hook invocations.
  6. **Resume is always manual**: `/codex-pair-resume` or `rm .codex-pair/state/paused`. No expiry logic — the user may have their own reasons to stay paused; the reset hint is informational. This was an explicit user decision during design (auto-resume rejected).
- **Alternatives rejected:** (a) A dedicated `state/quota-paused` sentinel alongside `state/paused` — two files to reason about, and the skill/dashboard already knows one sentinel; (b) auto-resume on the parsed reset time — clobbers user intent without their knowledge; (c) widening the `VERDICT_PREFIXES` taxonomy with a `quota` verdict — the verdict set is a closed enum consumed by log tooling; (d) a new `lib/auto-pause.mjs` module — pause ownership belongs to `state.mjs` (the file that knows the sentinel path), splitting it creates an import cycle risk and a second place to maintain.
- **Reference:** `docs/plans/2026-06-11-codex-pair-auto-pause-design.md` and `docs/plans/2026-06-11-codex-pair-auto-pause-plan.md`

## ADR-119: Inline-bundle `@ask-llm/shared` via tsdown — retire `bundledDependencies` + prepack lifecycle (fixes #115, resolves ADR-107's Tier-B question)

- **Date:** 2026-06-10
- **Status:** Accepted (implemented on `fix/115-inline-bundle-shared` 2026-06-10; resolves the "B1 vs B2" question ADR-107 deferred; supersedes ADR-052's mechanism while preserving its motivation)
- **Context:** Issue #115 (`npm install -g ask-llm-mcp` → `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on Node 26) is caused by npm 11's global-install handling of `bundledDependencies`: bundled packages extract, but their non-bundled transitive deps become empty placeholder directories (78 in the ADR-106 reproduction). The bundling exists only because `@ask-llm/shared` is `private: true` (ADR-052). The first fix attempt (ADR-106 — publish shared as public, B2) shipped four broken tarballs on 2026-05-27 because it verified via `yarn pack` while the actual publish path (`changesets/action` → `npm publish`) never rewrites `workspace:*`; ADR-107 reverted it and left "B1 (inline-bundle) vs B2 (public shared, properly sequenced)" as deferred Tier-B work. Re-evaluation (2026-06-09/10) surfaced the decisive asymmetry: **B2 does not remove the publish-time rewrite** — even with shared public, the `npm publish` path still ships `workspace:*` literally unless a prepack-style rewrite survives, and B2 additionally requires creating the `@ask-llm` npm org and accepting shared-before-dependents publish ordering — the exact failure surfaces of the 2026-05-27 incident. B1, by inlining shared into each package's `dist/`, removes shared from `dependencies` entirely: nothing to bundle (kills the npm-11 #115 class) and nothing to rewrite (kills the npm-9 `EUNSUPPORTEDPROTOCOL` class of ADR-052) — both bug classes lose their preconditions instead of being guarded.
- **Decision:** Convert the five publishable packages (`ask-gemini-mcp`, `ask-codex-mcp`, `ask-ollama-mcp`, `ask-antigravity-mcp`, `ask-llm-mcp`) to build with **tsdown** (`format: esm`, `target: node20`, `dts: true`, `clean: true`, `deps: { alwaysBundle: ['@ask-llm/shared'] }`; multi-entry chunk splitting is always-on in rolldown), with entries mirroring the existing `exports` map (`.`, `./executor`, `./register`, bin `cli`). Tooling note: tsup was the original pick (2026-06-10 morning); the maintainer redirected to **tsdown** the same day — tsup is in maintenance mode and tsdown (rolldown + oxc) is its designated successor. tsdown requires **Node ≥22.18 at build time** (emitted output still targets node20, so consumer `engines: >=20` is unchanged); consequence: the CI `test` matrix moves `[20.x, 22.x]` → `[22.x, 24.x]`, with Node-20 *runtime* support verified by installing + booting the packed tarballs on a Node 20 leg of the new smoke job — the tsdown-documented pattern for supporting runtimes older than the build toolchain. `@ask-llm/shared` stays `private: true`, keeps building with `tsc`, and moves from `dependencies` to **`devDependencies: workspace:*`** in each MCP (local resolution + changesets cascade; devDeps are ignored by npm on install and were always excluded from the preflight gate's scan). `bundledDependencies` and the `prepack`/`postpack` hooks are deleted from all five packages; `scripts/prepack-bundle.mjs` + `scripts/postpack-restore.mjs` are deleted. `ask-llm-mcp`'s four sibling provider deps **remain runtime `dependencies`** (its `index.ts` loads executors via string-variable dynamic `import()` — verified un-inlinable and correctly left verbatim by the bundler) and switch from `workspace:*` to **real semver ranges** maintained by changesets (`updateInternalDependencies: "patch"` already configured; yarn 4 still links workspace copies locally) — so no `workspace:` protocol survives anywhere in any published manifest with zero publish-time rewriting. The preflight gate (`preflight-no-workspace-protocol.mjs`) is kept, simplified to a static manifest scan (no lifecycle to fire anymore), and **strengthened to also fail on any `bundledDependencies` field** — structurally locking out both regression classes. **Verification reverses the ADR-106 gap:** reproduce #115 first on a local Node 26 (nvm) against the currently-published version, then prove the fixed tarballs install + boot on the same binary via `npm pack`/`npm install -g` (never `yarn pack`), plus a **permanent Node 26 CI smoke** — provider tarballs installed globally directly; `ask-llm-mcp` via a throwaway local registry (verdaccio) because its freshly-bumped sibling ranges may not exist on the real registry at PR time. **Must-verify (implementation Task 1):** that the shared→MCP changesets cascade still fires across a devDependency edge (the published artifacts *embed* shared, so a shared fix must keep triggering MCP republishes); fallback if not: a CI guard requiring changesets touching `packages/shared/src/**` to include the five publishable packages. Claude-plugin is untouched (private, marketplace-distributed, keeps `workspace:*`; its static `ask-*-mcp/executor` imports keep working because subpath exports are preserved). Spec: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-10-inline-bundle-shared-design.md`](superpowers/specs/2026-06-10-inline-bundle-shared-design.md). **Implementation outcomes (2026-06-10):** the cascade must-verify FAILED — `updateInternalDependents: "always"` does not traverse devDependency edges (empirically: a shared-only changeset bumped only shared + claude-plugin, none of the five MCPs) — so the fallback CI guard `scripts/check-shared-changeset.mjs` is now load-bearing (wired into ci.yml, requires shared changes to changeset all five MCPs). Two more empirical findings: (1) #115 reproduction requires a COLD npm cache (`npm cache clean --force`) — a warm cache masks the bug entirely, so all verification paths cache-clean first; (2) verdaccio's `'**' proxy: npmjs` rejected local publishes at npmjs-existing versions — the smoke registry needs an `'ask-*'` no-proxy block so the PR's tarballs always win; (3) **post-merge release aftershock:** the first Version Packages PR after the semver-range switch broke the publish run — changesets bumps llm-mcp's caret ranges in package.json but never touches `yarn.lock` (under `workspace:*` the lock descriptor was version-invariant, so this class was unreachable before), and `release.yml`'s `yarn install --immutable` correctly refused the stale lockfile *before* the publish step (also before the loud-on-failure guard, which only watches the changesets step — hence no auto-issue). Fixed by adding `yarn install --mode=update-lockfile` to the `changeset:version` script, so every future Version Packages PR carries its own lockfile update; `--immutable` stays everywhere else — it is the guard that caught this.
- **Consequences:** **Gains:** (a) #115 fixed at the architecture level — `npm install -g` / `npx -y` work on Node 26 because no `bundledDependencies` exists to trigger npm 11's placeholder bug; (b) the ADR-052/106/107 rewrite saga ends — no publish-time manifest mutation at all, so the "verified the wrong publisher" failure class has nothing left to break; (c) two fewer lifecycle scripts and a simpler, stronger preflight gate; (d) self-contained tarballs with rolled-up `.d.ts` (no dangling `@ask-llm/shared` type imports — acceptance-checked). **Costs:** (a) a new build devDependency (tsdown) and per-package `tsdown.config.ts`; type-checking stays on `tsc --noEmit` in `lint`, tests stay on vitest against `src`; (b) each MCP's dist embeds its own copy of shared — cross-package module-state sharing is no longer possible (verified safe today: executors exchange only plain data; no `toolRegistry` crosses package boundaries; rolldown's always-on multi-entry chunk splitting keeps state singleton *within* each package across its entries); a future feature relying on cross-package shared singletons must use the filesystem (as chunkCache/sessions already do); (c) shared fixes reach users only through MCP republishes (cascade must keep firing — the Task-1 must-verify); (d) `dist/` output shape changes (flat entries + chunks), so the `exports`/`bin` maps change with it; (e) the CI build toolchain now requires Node ≥22.18 (tsdown), dropping the Node-20 build leg — Node-20 support remains verified at the artifact level (tarball install + boot smoke). **Verification methodology:** all packaging claims validated through `npm` (pack/install -g/doctor on Node 26), never `yarn pack`, per the ADR-107 postscript; the Node 26 smoke becomes permanent CI so the regression class cannot silently return.

## ADR-118: codex-pair Stop-gate — opt-in `blockOn: HIGH` turn-end gate, log-derived + reconciled

- **Date:** 2026-06-09
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on branch `feat/codex-pair-stop-gate` (closes #142). Re-introduces a `Stop` hook that ADR-048 removed, on a fundamentally different mechanism.
- **Context:** codex-pair's per-edit findings (`PostToolUse` → `.codex-pair/log.jsonl`) are **advisory only**; in a long session HIGH findings get silently ignored (issue #142 field repro: ~250 HIGH lines, none engaged). ADR-048 removed the old `Stop` hook because it ran a **fresh `gemini -p` review every turn** (latency, quota burn, overlapping WIP reviews) and used `git diff HEAD` (silently skipped untracked files). The challenge: make HIGH un-ignorable **without** repeating those failures.
- **Decision:** A new `Stop` hook (`codex-pair-stop-gate.mjs`) that blocks turn-end while unaddressed HIGH findings remain. Key choices: **(1) Opt-in, default OFF** via `blockOn: HIGH` in `.codex-pair/context.md` frontmatter — no surprise blocking for existing users (the ADR-048 lesson); a blocking gate is `kano:reverse` for some, so make it opt-in. **(2) Log-derived, zero new LLM calls** — reads already-computed `log.jsonl` findings, not a fresh review. **(3) Reconcile the append-only log against present reality**, since a file's gate-status changes via channels the log never sees: `[A]` skip files not on disk (deletion/rename), `[C]` if a file's *latest* entry verdict is indeterminate (`skipped`/`error`/`retried`/`broker_fallback`) fail-open for it (don't fall back to stale HIGH), `[B]` skip files clean-vs-HEAD via `git status --porcelain` (revert/branch-switch) — an additive *subtractive* filter, NOT ADR-048's scoping (porcelain lists untracked too, so no blind spot). **(4) File-scoped content-hash acks** — `hashConcernBody(relPath + ":" + text)` (shared with ADR-096's repetition tracker); `/codex-pair-ack <hash> "<reason>"` records to `.codex-pair/state/acks.json`. **(5) Fail-open & loud** — any hook error warns to stderr and exits 0; a gate that blocks turns on its own bug is worse than no gate. The design was refined by an external critique (Antigravity) that surfaced the A/B/C/E edge cases. Pure logic lives in `scripts/lib/stop-gate.mjs` (unit-tested I/O-injected); the hook is thin wiring.
- **Consequences:** Opt-in users get HIGH findings gated at turn-end with zero extra LLM cost; everyone else is unchanged (advisory + ADR-096 in-turn escalation). Why this isn't ADR-048: no LLM calls (reads the log), opt-in (not unsolicited), per-turn firing is the *desired* gate semantic. Out of scope (follow-up ADRs): `open.json` ledger, edit-significance gating, debounce, MED/LOW gating, default-ON, concern-text normalization for LLM wording drift, `--no-cache` ack-clear for cross-file fixes. Known low-risk limitation: a project located under a symlinked path (e.g. `/var`) could under-gate because the `[B]` git filter compares raw vs realpath'd paths — fail-safe (never over-blocks). `@ask-llm/plugin` is `private` → no changeset.

## ADR-117: Codex quota fallback for CLI 0.137+ — surface stdout-borne errors + `usage limit` signal

- **Date:** 2026-06-09
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on branch `fix/codex-usage-limit-quota-detection` (shared `commandExecutor` + `sanitizeErrorForLLM`, codex `QUOTA_SIGNALS`, exported `isQuotaError`, smoke `QUOTA_PATTERN`, tests; shared + codex suites green). Recurrence of the ADR-044 (Gemini quota-format drift) pattern, now for Codex.
- **Context:** Discovered while pushing a docs-only commit — the local pre-push codex smoke (ADR-043) hard-failed instead of skip-with-warning. Root cause: Codex CLI `0.137.0` reports plan exhaustion as `{"type":"error","message":"You've hit your usage limit. ... try again at <date>"}` on **stdout JSONL** while exiting non-zero, emitting only a benign `Reading additional input from stdin...` notice on **stderr**. Two stale layers hid this: (1) `executeCommand`'s non-zero-exit branch used `stderr.trim() || "Unknown error"` — a short-circuit that **discards stdout**, so the quota text never reached `isQuotaError()`; (2) codex's `QUOTA_SIGNALS` (`out of credits`, `spend cap`, `429`, …, tuned for codex 0.134 per #127) didn't include the 0.137 `usage limit` phrasing. Net effect: the gpt-5.5 → gpt-5.5-mini fallback **silently never fired** for real users hitting their plan limit — they got a hard error — and the smoke gate blocked unrelated pushes. The earlier BUGS.md entry framed this as a generic "live codex CLI fails locally" DX nuisance; the refined root cause is a user-facing reliability regression.
- **Decision:** Four coordinated changes. (1) **`commandExecutor`** (shared): on non-zero exit, **union** both streams — `[stderr.trim(), stdout.trim()].filter(Boolean).join("\n")` — instead of stderr-or-stdout, so stdout-borne errors (codex JSONL; any future provider) stay visible to downstream detection. (2) **`sanitizeErrorForLLM`** (shared): add `usage limit` to `QUOTA_PASSTHROUGH_PATTERNS` so the quota text survives the 3-line/500-char truncation and reaches `isQuotaError`. (3) **codex `QUOTA_SIGNALS`**: add `usage limit`; **export `isQuotaError`** for direct unit testing (mirrors gemini's exported `classifyGeminiCliError`). (4) **smoke `QUOTA_PATTERN`**: add `usage limit` so the quota-skip escape (ADR-051) fires for this format. Rejected alternatives: (a) a codex-only fix that reconstructs stdout via the `onProgress` callback — hackier and wouldn't surface the quota text in the thrown error for the smoke grep; (b) making the union stdout-first — stderr usually carries the real cause for gemini/ollama, so stderr leads.
- **Consequences:** Codex quota exhaustion now triggers the gpt-5.5-mini fallback as designed, and the smoke gate skips-with-warning on this format. The `commandExecutor` change is behavioral for **all** providers (gemini/ollama error messages now include stdout context on failure, truncated by `sanitizeErrorForLLM`) — hence `@ask-llm/shared` + `ask-codex-mcp` both bump (patch), cascading a republish to the dependent MCPs. Existing real-spawn `commandExecutor` tests are unaffected (they exit 0); two new tests pin the stdout-union behavior and one pins the `usage limit` passthrough; a new `quota-detection.test.ts` covers `isQuotaError` across the new and pre-existing signals. Future maintenance: when Codex changes its quota phrasing again, add the new substring to `QUOTA_SIGNALS` + `QUOTA_PASSTHROUGH_PATTERNS` + smoke `QUOTA_PATTERN` (the same three-point pattern ADR-044 established for Gemini).

## ADR-116: Antigravity default model — Gemini 3.5 Flash (High) via `--model` (supersedes ADR-114's "no model selection")

- **Date:** 2026-06-08
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on branch `feat/antigravity-default-model-flash` (constants + executor + tool + orchestrator label + docs + tests; antigravity-mcp + llm-mcp suites green). Narrows the ADR-114 "no `model` param" decision.
- **Context:** ADR-114 shipped `ask-antigravity-mcp` with **no model selection**, citing "`-m` model-switching hangs under `-p`". That was a true finding about the **short `-m` flag**, but it ossified into a blanket "no model selection" claim across six surfaces (executor comment, tool description, package README, provider docs ×2, spec). A fresh empirical probe of the installed `agy` 1.0.6 found a documented long **`--model`** flag plus an `agy models` subcommand, and a live one-shot test — `agy -p --model "Gemini 3.5 Flash (Low)" "…" --print-timeout 120s --dangerously-skip-permissions --sandbox` — returned cleanly with exit 0, **no hang**. `agy models` lists Gemini 3.5 Flash (Low/Medium/High), Gemini 3.1 Pro (Low/High), Claude Sonnet 4.6 (Thinking), Claude Opus 4.6 (Thinking), GPT-OSS 120B. The maintainer wants Gemini 3.5 Flash as the default for its coding strength.
- **Decision:** Add model selection. Resolution order: explicit `options.model` → `ASK_ANTIGRAVITY_MODEL` env → `MODELS.DEFAULT = "Gemini 3.5 Flash (High)"` (the High effort tier, chosen for code-review quality). `buildArgs` appends `--model <model>` (the verbatim `agy models` display string) after `--add-dir`; the executor returns the resolved model so `AskResponse.model` reports the real model instead of the placeholder `"antigravity"` label. The orchestrator's `defaultModel` label is synced to `"Gemini 3.5 Flash (High)"`. The short `-m` flag is **never** used (it still hangs). Multi-turn / sessions stay out of scope (antigravity-cli #7).
- **Consequences:** Antigravity calls now run on Gemini 3.5 Flash (High) by default, overridable per-environment via `ASK_ANTIGRAVITY_MODEL` or per-call via `options.model`. The six "no model selection" surfaces are corrected. The historical ADR-114 + spec v1 records stay as-is (accurate about `-m`); this ADR records the `-m` vs `--model` distinction so the constraint isn't re-introduced. `ask-antigravity-mcp` gets a **minor** bump (new feature) and republishes; the cascade republishes `ask-llm-mcp`. Risk: if `agy` renames its model display strings a pinned value could 404 — mitigated by the env override and the `agy models` pointer in the docs.

## ADR-114: New `ask-antigravity-mcp` Provider — Experimental, Transcript-Scraping (stdout-first)

- **Date:** 2026-06-07
- **Status:** Accepted — implemented on branch `feat/antigravity-mcp-package` (commits 205ef87…5a4747b) via subagent-driven TDD (9 tasks, per-task + final holistic review, all suites green); pending real-`agy` validation (spec §10) and merge. Realizes the deferred ADR-113 "P1 (Antigravity `agy` provider)".
- **Context:** Post-2026-06-18, the surviving `gemini` paths are paid `GEMINI_API_KEY` (metered) and Code Assist Standard/Enterprise — both unattractive for a flat-rate **subscription** "second opinion" workflow. But Antigravity (`agy`) **is covered by the same Google AI Pro/Ultra subscription** (Pro 5×/Ultra 20× rate limits), so the subscription's value relocates to `agy` rather than dying. We want that value reachable through the ask-llm toolchain. **Add a new provider package, not migrate `gemini-mcp`** (which still serves enterprise/API-key users). Hard constraint: `agy`'s headless `-p` is broken for subprocess use — empty stdout in non-TTY (gemini-cli #27466), no `--output-format` JSON, no capturable session id (`--conversation` is resume-only, antigravity-cli #7), and `-m` model-switching hangs under `-p`. The only working response path is reading `agy`'s internal transcript files (community MCP-bridge precedent). `agy` is not installable on the dev/CI machine, so the design must be defensive and fully mockable.
- **Decision:** Build `ask-antigravity-mcp` (`packages/antigravity-mcp`, mirrors `codex-mcp`) **now**, labeled **experimental**, using **Approach B: stdout-first, transcript-fallback** — a "response source" chain `[parseJsonStdout(future), parsePlainStdout, scrapeTranscript(sinceMs)]`, first non-null wins. The fragile file-scraping is isolated in `transcriptReader.ts` (`readLatestResponse(sinceMs): string | null`, never throws). v1 surface = one-shot `ask-antigravity` + `ping`, `includeDirs` → repeated `--add-dir` (verified flag), **no** `model` param, **no** sessions/changeMode. Calls are **serialized via an in-process mutex** (correctness — concurrent runs race on `cache/last_conversations.json` + the newest-`brain`-dir heuristic). Safety: default `--dangerously-skip-permissions` + `--sandbox` (escape hatch `ASK_ANTIGRAVITY_SANDBOX=0`) + a read-only prompt preamble. Integrated into the `ask-llm-mcp` orchestrator, availability-gated on `isCommandAvailable('agy')`. Plugin agent/skill/brainstorm wiring deferred. Rejected: Approach A (pure scraper — no self-heal when #27466 is fixed); Approach C (caller-pinned conversation id — impossible per #7).
- **Consequences:** Restores subscription-backed second opinions through the tool for `agy` users, while `gemini-mcp` stays intact. Approach B self-heals onto stdout the moment upstream fixes #27466 (or adds `--output-format json`) with zero migration. Primary risk: `agy` transcript layout changes (`agy 1.0.5` already dual-writes `.db`) — contained by the isolated reader, a schema-changed error, and a `.db`-only fixture test. Not published to npm until validated against a real `agy`. Open verification items (stdin support, exact transcript schema, `--sandbox` vs `--add-dir`, `ping` authed-check) tracked in the spec.
- **Design doc:** [docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-07-ask-antigravity-mcp-design.md](superpowers/specs/2026-06-07-ask-antigravity-mcp-design.md)

## ADR-113: Gemini Tier-Discontinuation Guidance — Date-Gated, Class-Based Enricher (#140 P0)

- **Date:** 2026-06-05
- **Status:** Accepted (branch `feat/gemini-tier-discontinuation`, #140 P0).
- **Context:** Gemini CLI stops serving free/Pro/Ultra accounts on 2026-06-18 (verified against the Google Developers Blog announcement + corroborating press); Code Assist Standard/Enterprise exempt; the successor is a separate closed-source `agy` binary. `ask-gemini-mcp` wraps the `gemini` subprocess, so the dominant post-cutoff failure is a runtime auth/quota error (the binary still installs and launches). The exact error string is unknown until a real user hits it on 2026-06-19.
- **Decision:** A date-gated, class-based terminal-error enricher. A pure `classifyGeminiCliError` (raw error → `workspaceTrust | quota | tierAccess | operational | unknown`, word-boundary matched) + a pure `formatTierNote` that **prepends** a hedged notice when `now ≥ 2026-06-18T00:00:00Z` (UTC, `ASK_GEMINI_TIER_CUTOFF`-overridable, invalid→default) and the class is `quota`/`tierAccess`. Wired at the executor's two terminal throws — the **raw Flash error** class at the fallback-failure site (Pro is quota there by construction), the **raw error** class at the else site. The narrow `QUOTA_PATTERNS` Flash-fallback trigger is **untouched**, so auth errors enrich with no wasted Flash call and enterprise quota→Flash is preserved. The note is prepended (not appended) for visibility in MCP clients and is idempotent via a stable marker. P1 (Antigravity `agy` provider, CI version-pin) + P2 (ask-llm-mcp degraded-probe, sponsorship) deferred.
- **Consequences:** Closes #140 P0. **Dual external review (Codex + Gemini, both "ship with changes") shaped the design** and is recorded in `docs/plans/2026-06-05-gemini-tier-discontinuation-design.md`: classify raw errors not the composed fallback string (Codex — avoids a "quota" false-positive when Flash fails on a timeout); prioritize `403`/`PERMISSION_DENIED` (both); prepend for visibility + explicit `agy`-not-supported wording (Gemini). The multi-LLM debate caught a real correctness bug before any code existed — the project's "debate the plan" thesis in action. Loose tier terms (subscription/Standard/…) can over-match a genuine enterprise error post-cutoff, mitigated by date-gating + hedged "likely caused by" wording. Verified by pure-unit + `vi.mock`-based executor catch-flow tests; no live cutoff test is possible (the env override is the manual lever). **Side-finding (separate, to verify):** the configured `MODELS.FLASH` (`gemini-3.5-flash`) returned `404 ModelNotFoundError` on gemini-cli 0.44.1 during this session while `gemini-3-flash-preview` worked — a possible fallback-model misconfiguration tracked in `docs/BUGS.md`, independent of #140.

## ADR-112: codex-pair Edit-Debounce — Detached Delayed-Worker (Resolves the #96 Edit-Debounce Deferral)

- **Date:** 2026-06-04
- **Status:** Accepted (branch `feat/codex-pair-edit-debounce` → PR #144, closes #96). Numbering note: ADR 110–111 were consumed by parallel `ask-codex-edit` work that is not (yet) on `main`; this entry stays **112** to avoid a future collision, leaving an intentional gap.
- **Context:** #96 Bug 2 / Idea 1 asks to collapse a burst of rapid same-file edits into a single review of the *settled* state (cuts Codex spend ~⅓; kills the false-alarm class where a review flags a transient state the next edit corrects). It was previously **deferred** (#96; ADR-109's "Block 5 — codex-pair reliability"; `docs/BUGS.md`): a non-blocking per-edit hook can't "wait for edits to settle," so a true debounce needs a process that outlives a single edit. The broker (`codex app-server`) is Codex's own process and off-by-default (`ASK_CODEX_BROKER=1`), so it cannot host the timer for the default path. Brainstormed + planned in `docs/plans/2026-06-03-codex-pair-edit-debounce-{design,plan}.md`.
- **Decision:** A **detached delayed-worker**, no daemon. On each edit the hook (`codex-pair-watch.mjs`) bumps a per-file `generation` record under `.codex-pair/state/debounce/` and spawns a detached `codex-pair-debounce-worker.mjs` carrying that generation, then exits without reviewing inline. The worker sleeps the settle window (`debounceMs`, default 15s) and reviews only if it is still the latest generation (trailing-edge) or the burst exceeded the cap (`debounceMaxMs`, default 60s — anti-starvation). To review, the worker **re-invokes the hook in `CODEX_PAIR_FORCE_SYNC=1` mode**, reusing the entire existing pipeline (cache → inflight-lock → codex → parse → log → repetition shard), captures the emitted verdict from the hook's stdout, and queues it in `.codex-pair/state/pending/`. Verdicts surface on the next PostToolUse edit drain **or** a new `UserPromptSubmit` drain at the start of the next user turn (red-team addition — closes the single-edit-then-stop gap; chosen over `Stop`, which fires before the ~15s review completes, and avoids ADR-048's removed-Stop-hook baggage). The per-file inflight lock acquired by the forced-sync hook is the atomic claim — the worker holds no lock (which would deadlock against the hook). ON by default; `debounceMs:0` (or `CODEX_PAIR_FORCE_SYNC=1`) restores the v0.7.0 synchronous behavior verbatim. `SessionEnd` clears debounce+pending state (un-gated by the broker flag) so orphaned sleepers self-cancel.
- **Consequences:** Closes #96. Surfacing is deferred (the worker has no stdout to Claude) but reliably reaches Claude via the PostToolUse or UserPromptSubmit drain; only a terminal turn with no further edit *and* no further prompt leaves a verdict log-only. Reuse-by-re-invocation avoids extracting the 1188-line hook's review pipeline, at the cost of a stdout-scraping contract (`extractSystemMessage`) between worker and hook. Test hygiene: pre-debounce behavioral tests pin `ASK_CODEX_DEBOUNCE_MS=0` so they keep their synchronous assumption. **Validated:** 8-task TDD build (39 new tests; full plugin suite 354 green + lint/types clean) plus a **live smoke against real codex** — a 3-edit burst produced exactly one `verdict:none` review of the settled file (gen-1/gen-2 workers bowed out), the queued verdict drained through `UserPromptSubmit` as `additionalContext`, and `debounceMs:0` reviewed synchronously inline. The earlier "belongs in the broker" framing is narrowed: the timer belongs in *a* process outliving the edit, which the detached worker provides without a daemon.

## ADR-109: Upstream-Issue Consolidation — 21 Issues → 5 Fix Blocks, Audit-Cadence Retirement

- **Date:** 2026-05-30
- **Status:** Accepted (branch `fix/block-1-parser-defensiveness`; Block 1 shipped, Blocks 2–5 pending)

**Context.** The open-issue tracker had grown to 21, of which **19 were a self-authored "upstream-CLI audit" cadence** (one issue every 1–3 days since 2026-04-21, each a "follow-up to" the last, the majority concluding *"no breaking changes"*). The remaining 2 were codex-pair feature reports. The audits had already diagnosed their own problem (#28: *"drain the backlog instead of recutting it"*; #39: *"cut the consolidated PR"*) and the `ROADMAP.md` already held the latent fix taxonomy (Tiers C–F). The defect was a **process artifact**, not 19 distinct bugs: no canonical upstream-compat tracker, so every re-audit spawned a new tracked issue.

**Decision.** Consolidate by **root cause, not by audit**, after verifying each audit claim directly against the executors/constants (audits are secondary sources):

1. **Close immediately (6):** verified-already-shipped (#27 trust-env co-emit `geminiExecutor.ts:421-424`; #35 reasoning-token mapping `codexExecutor.ts:65,77`) as `completed`; pure-noise cadence check-ins (#24/#25/#28/#39) as `not planned`, folded into the roadmap.
2. **Fix the remaining 15 as 5 root-cause story blocks**, each gated by `/multi-review` per fix + live smoke per block, closed via its fix PR:
   - Block 1 — parser event-coverage defensiveness (#57/#114/#116/#117)
   - Block 2 — quota/fallback freshness (#127/#131)
   - Block 3 — flag/contract drift cleanup incl. the verified-live `--full-auto` plugin residual (#37/#38/#52/#54/#75)
   - Block 4 — capability adoption (#59/#102)
   - Block 5 — codex-pair reliability (#74/#96)
3. **Retire the cadence:** future upstream deltas become a comment on the standing roadmap, not a new tracked issue (per the ROADMAP meta-observation: stop re-cutting when the backlog is unchanged ≥48h).

Roadmap/tracker: [`docs/plans/2026-05-30-upstream-issue-consolidation.md`](plans/2026-05-30-upstream-issue-consolidation.md).

**Block 1 (shipped this ADR).** Both JSONL/stream-json parsers used `if`-chains with no default branch, silently dropping new upstream event types. Fixes: codex `turn.failed` branch extracting `error.message` so failed/quota turns propagate and trigger fallback (instead of returning the raw JSONL dump); codex `error` events surface `parsed.message` not the JSON envelope; gemini stream parser logs unrecognized event types via `Logger.debug` against a `RECOGNIZED_STREAM_EVENT_TYPES` set. TDD (4 RED→GREEN), full suites green (codex 49 / gemini 109), `/multi-review` clean (both providers, zero findings), live smoke green.

**Consequences.** Tracker drops 21→15 immediately and trends to `open issues == open work`. The verification step caught that the audit's "`JSON.stringify` breaks `isQuotaError`" claim was overstated (substring matching still matched) — the real quota gap was `turn.failed` being unhandled; fixing the right defect required checking the claim, not trusting it.

## ADR-108: Internal Bug-Hunt Sweep (2026-05-29) + Correction to the `workspace:*` Alarm

- **Date:** 2026-05-29
- **Status:** Accepted (branch `fix/bug-hunt-2026-05-29`; not yet published)
- **Context:** A repo-wide defensive bug hunt was run on a clean tree. Methodology: 8 parallel review agents partitioned by subsystem (gemini executor, shared core, shared state/cache, changeMode, codex+ollama, llm-mcp orchestrator, plugin/release scripts, codex-pair broker), each reading its files in full and reporting confidence-scored findings; **every finding was then independently re-verified by reading the code (and, for the npm claim, by running real installs) before any fix.** Fixes followed strict TDD (failing test first, watched fail, then minimal fix). This entry records both the fixes and — more importantly for institutional memory — a **correction** to the single highest-severity finding, which was a false positive.
- **Decision / outcomes:**
  1. **CORRECTION — `workspace:*` in published manifests is cosmetic, NOT a breakage.** The hunt's loudest finding claimed the live npm packages (`ask-gemini-mcp@1.6.7`, `ask-codex-mcp@0.3.9`, `ask-ollama-mcp@0.3.4`, `ask-llm-mcp@0.3.10`) were still broken because `npm view <pkg> dependencies` shows literal `workspace:*` (a consequence of `postpack` restoring the manifest before npm snapshots the registry packument). **This is wrong as a *breakage* claim.** The packument also carries `bundleDependencies` (verified: `npm view ask-llm-mcp@0.3.10 bundleDependencies` → `["@ask-llm/shared","ask-gemini-mcp","ask-codex-mcp","ask-ollama-mcp"]`), so npm satisfies those deps from the copies bundled inside the tarball and **never resolves the `workspace:*` spec against the registry** (and indeed `@ask-llm/shared` is unpublished — `E404` — which is fine precisely because it is bundled). Empirically confirmed by real installs into a temp dir: `npm install ask-llm-mcp@0.3.10` succeeds identically on **npm 9.8.1, 10.9.2, and 11.14.1** (93 packages, `@ask-llm/shared` present). The earlier ADR-107 recovery works as designed. **Guidance for future sessions: do not re-flag `workspace:*` in `npm view` output as a regression — it is cosmetic given `bundleDependencies`.** Residual (genuine but low): install-safety rests entirely on `bundleDependencies`; a future refactor that strips the bundle while keeping `workspace:*` (the ADR-106 failure mode) would break installs — the ADR-107 preflight gate guards the source manifest but does not inspect the published packument, so a packument-level assertion (`npm view … dependencies` clean OR `bundleDependencies` present) would be a strictly stronger future safeguard. Not implemented now (no active regression).
  2. **Nine bugs fixed (all TDD'd, full suite + lint + types green):** (a) 🔴 changeMode legacy parser infinite loop — `continue` without advancing the regex iterator (`changeModeParser.ts`); (b) 🟠 REPL hang on Ctrl-D/EOF — `readline/promises` `question()` never settles on close, fixed by racing against the `close` event in a new `runReplLoop`/`questionOrEof` (`repl.ts`); (c) 🟠 chunkCache cross-process corruption — non-atomic write + reader deleting unparseable files, fixed with temp+rename and no-unlink-on-parse (`chunkCache.ts`); (d) 🟠 broker orphaned-socket lockout — unlink stale socket before bind, path-gated (`broker-lifecycle.mjs`); (e) 🟠 broker post-handshake hang not falling back — tag transport/timeout RPC failures with `brokerFailure` (`broker.mjs`), upholding ADR-077; (f) 🟡 SIGKILL grace timer leak — track/`unref`/clear it (`commandExecutor.ts`); (g) 🟡 `file:`→`@` over-match — anchored regex `convertFileSyntaxToAt` (`geminiExecutor.ts`); (h) 🟡 `enforceLimit` TOCTOU — guard per-file `statSync` (`sessions.ts`, `chunkCache.ts`); (i) 🟡 `saveSession` silent write failure (see #3).
  3. **Minor API change:** `saveSession()` now returns `boolean` (was `void`) and `appendAndSaveSession()` returns an added `persisted: boolean`, so a failed write is no longer reported as success. Additive for existing consumers; wiring each executor to *act* on `persisted` (e.g. not advertise an unsaved session id) is deferred follow-up.
  4. **Intentionally NOT changed:** ollama `isModelNotFoundError` matching bare `"not found"` — `ollamaExecutor.test.ts` encodes this as the intended fallback trigger, so tightening it would contradict the project's own contract for a marginal (`OLLAMA_HOST`-misconfigured 404) edge case.
- **Consequences:** Two reachable hangs (parser, REPL) and a cross-process data-loss race (chunkCache) eliminated; the codex-pair broker no longer self-bricks on an orphaned socket and honours the ADR-077 fallback contract on a mid-session hang. Test count grew (+~25 across the touched packages). The corrected `workspace:*` finding is the headline lesson: an agent's high-confidence "verified" only covers the commands it actually ran — the agent ran `npm view`/`--dry-run` but never an `npm install`, so its breakage *inference* was unverified; a single real install on npm 9 refuted it. Empirical discipline (verify against the actual code path, not a proxy) applied to a review finding, mirroring the ADR-106→107 publisher-path lesson.

## ADR-107: Restore `bundledDependencies` Architecture + Preflight Gate (Recovery from ADR-106 Regression)
- **Date:** 2026-05-28
- **Status:** Accepted (supersedes ADR-106's MECHANISM but preserves ADR-106's MOTIVATION as Tier-B deferred work; institutional-memory entry for the 2026-05-27 broken-release incident)
- **Context:** ADR-106 was a confident-but-wrong refactor. It removed `scripts/prepack-bundle.mjs` + `bundledDependencies` from all four MCP packages on the assumption that yarn 4 would auto-rewrite `workspace:*` → fixed-version through `changeset publish`. The assumption was empirically tested via `yarn workspace ask-llm-mcp pack` (which rewrote correctly), and that test was treated as sufficient verification. **It wasn't.** The actual publish path is `changeset publish` → `npm publish`, not `yarn npm publish`. Yarn's workspace-protocol rewrite only fires through yarn-native publish, never through npm publish. The verification step tested the wrong publisher. On 2026-05-27 ~21:57 UTC, the merged Version Packages PR triggered a real publish that produced four broken tarballs on the npm registry: `ask-gemini-mcp@1.6.6`, `ask-codex-mcp@0.3.8`, `ask-ollama-mcp@0.3.3`, `ask-llm-mcp@0.3.9` — all with literal `"workspace:*"` strings in their `dependencies` field. The fifth package, `@ask-llm/shared@0.3.2`, failed to publish entirely because the `@ask-llm` npm scope had never been created on the maintainer's npm account (another verification gap — the rollout assumed scope-creation as a side effect of first publish, which is wrong for org scopes). External users running `npm install ask-llm-mcp@0.3.9` immediately hit `EUNSUPPORTEDPROTOCOL: Unsupported URL Type "workspace:"` — the exact bug class [ADR-052](#adr-052-publish-time-rewrite-workspace--for-bundled-deps-fixes-npm-9-eunsupportedprotocol-and-deprecated-postpack-restore) was created to prevent. The maintainer pushed back on publishing shared as public BEFORE the broken publish happened ("we we need to publishe shared package? isn't it technical dependencies"); the verification step that should have validated their instinct (running `npm pack` instead of `yarn pack` against the changed configuration) never ran. **The architectural question this ADR resolves: how do we (a) restore service to the npm tarball line in minimal blast radius, (b) preserve the institutional memory of why ADR-106's mechanism didn't work, and (c) add a structural safeguard so the regression can't recur on a future refactor?**
- **Decision:** Three coordinated changes shipped in one PR (`fix/republish-workspace-protocol-regression`):
  1. **Restore the pre-#126 bundling architecture verbatim.** `scripts/prepack-bundle.mjs` (137 lines) and `scripts/postpack-restore.mjs` (70 lines) both restored from `git show 5ed5499^:scripts/...`. Each of the four MCP package.json files gets back its `prepack`/`postpack` script entries and `bundledDependencies` array. `@ask-llm/shared` gets back `"private": true` and its `publishConfig` block is removed (the metadata fields stay — they're harmless on a private package and reduce friction for a future, properly-sequenced public-shared migration). The LICENSE file added in PR #126 to `packages/shared/` is removed (no longer needed for a private package). Verified pre-deletion state: all four MCPs used `postpack` (not `postpublish`) as the cleanup hook; the `postpack` lifecycle was empirically validated by releases 1.5.7 through 1.6.5 (five working releases), so we restore exactly that hook rather than ADR-052's original `postpublish` design. The brainstorm coordinator initially flagged this as a concern ("1.5.6 tombstone lesson"), but file archaeology showed the `postpack` move happened in commit `aa0c048` and produced five subsequent successful releases — the lesson is that the cascade works when the bundling+prepack+postpack triple is consistent, regardless of which hook does cleanup. The 1.5.6 break was the *first* attempt at the restore-hook move, and was fixed in 1.5.7 before being load-bearing again.
  2. **Manual version bumps ahead of the broken-on-npm set.** Since the broken versions `1.6.6 / 0.3.8 / 0.3.3 / 0.3.9` are unrecoverable (npm doesn't allow republishing the same version), the hotfix must ship at strictly-greater numbers: `@ask-llm/shared@0.3.3` (still private, never published), `ask-gemini-mcp@1.6.7`, `ask-codex-mcp@0.3.9`, `ask-ollama-mcp@0.3.4`, `ask-llm-mcp@0.3.10`. The version edits go directly into each `package.json`; we pair them with a single changeset entry documenting the recovery (rather than letting changesets auto-bump from local pre-broken numbers, which would produce versions colliding with the broken-on-npm set — `1.6.5 → 1.6.6` would collide because npm has `1.6.6` already). Changesets' "publish-when-ahead-of-registry" semantic naturally handles this: with `1.6.7` in package.json and `1.6.6` on npm, the next publish run will pick up the hotfix automatically.
  3. **Preflight CI gate** at `scripts/preflight-no-workspace-protocol.mjs` — a Node script that runs `npm run prepack` for each publishable package, snapshots the rewritten `package.json` (mirroring what `npm pack` would actually publish), greps for `workspace:` literals in `dependencies`/`peerDependencies`/`optionalDependencies`/bundled-nested-manifests, restores via `postpack`, and exits 1 if any literal survived. Hooks into `.github/workflows/release.yml` as a step between `yarn test` and the `changesets/action` invocation — runs *before* anything reaches the registry. Locally invokable as `node scripts/preflight-no-workspace-protocol.mjs` so contributors can validate refactors of the bundling lifecycle without pushing. Verified the gate catches a regression: removing `prepack` from `gemini-mcp/package.json` makes the gate exit 1 with the exact dependency name surfaced (`dependencies["@ask-llm/shared"] = "workspace:*"`). Restoring fixes it. The gate is the structural safeguard that would have caught the 2026-05-27 incident before any tarball reached npm. **Out of scope explicitly:** (a) Closing #115 (Node 26 global install crash). The recovery deliberately does NOT solve #115 — that bug remains real, and the original ADR-106 motivation stands. #115 moves back to ROADMAP Tier B with two viable architectural paths that we now need to evaluate properly: **(B1) inline-bundling** via esbuild/tsup so each MCP's `dist/` is a single self-contained file with `@ask-llm/shared` inlined (drops the `bundledDependencies` mechanism entirely), or **(B2) public `@ask-llm/shared` after proper npm-scope setup** — manually create the `@ask-llm` org/scope on the maintainer's npm account, verify with a sandbox `0.0.0-test` publish from a feature branch, only then flip shared to public. Codex's brainstorm finding ruled out a hybrid "publish AND inline-bundle" approach: if shared is in `dependencies`, npm still installs it; if it's not, publishing shared is irrelevant — pick one mechanism, not both. (b) Migrating `release.yml` to use `yarn npm publish` instead of `npm publish`. Codex flagged this as technically viable (`yarn workspaces foreach -A --no-private -t npm publish --tolerate-republish` would auto-rewrite workspace:*), but it loses changesets/action's "only publish what's ahead-of-registry" semantic and would require restructuring the publish step. Larger blast radius than restoring the proven prepack mechanism. (c) Auto-recovery from future bad publishes via `npm unpublish` (npm only allows unpublish within 72h with caveats, and the policy was designed specifically to discourage it — `npm deprecate` is the canonical recovery for broken-but-stale versions). (d) Filing the npm 11 global install + `bundledDependencies` bug upstream — worth doing as a follow-up, but the ADR-107 architecture neither fixes nor depends on the upstream issue.
- **Consequences:** **Immediate (within hours of merge):** (a) Users currently hitting `EUNSUPPORTEDPROTOCOL` on `npm install ask-llm-mcp@latest` get a working `@latest` again at versions `1.6.7 / 0.3.9 / 0.3.4 / 0.3.10`. (b) The four broken versions get `npm deprecate`'d *after* the new versions publish, with a deprecation message pointing to the new versions and the incident issue. (c) The preflight gate runs on every future release, making this regression class structurally unrepeatable. **What we gain by going through this incident:** (a) The brainstorm-coordinator multi-LLM consensus pattern earned its keep — the Codex + Claude Opus convergence on Option A (with Gemini unavailable, so 2 reasoners) was the correct call; the maintainer's instinct that publishing shared was unnecessary was empirically validated by the publish failure. (b) The "verify against the actual publisher path" lesson now has a concrete artifact: ADR-106's verification step ran `yarn workspace ask-llm-mcp pack`, but the real publish path is `npm publish`; the preflight gate runs `npm` directly and catches the wrong-publisher mismatch class. (c) The maintainer-bot's `release-broken` label cycle, the README release badge, and the failure-tracking workflow step (PR #123) all earned their keep here — the workflow ran-failed-and-surfaced on the same day, no week-long silent failures. (d) ADR-106 stays in DECISIONS.md as institutional memory rather than being deleted (per the project's pattern: "superseded ADRs stay in place as the project's institutional memory") — a postscript is added pointing to ADR-107 and documenting the verification gap that caused the incident, so a future contributor reading ADR-106 in isolation can't repeat the same mistake. **What we lose / costs:** (a) `bundledDependencies` is back, which means the npm 11 global-install bug class (issue #115) is back too. Tier-B work (B1 inline-bundle vs B2 public-shared with proper scope setup) has to actually happen at some point — it's the only path to closing #115. (b) Five npm versions are now "stale" in semver-history terms: `ask-gemini-mcp@1.6.6`, `ask-codex-mcp@0.3.8`, `ask-ollama-mcp@0.3.3`, `ask-llm-mcp@0.3.9`, and `@ask-llm/shared@0.3.2` (the one that never actually published — but its CHANGELOG entry exists in the repo history). These are gaps in the version line for future contributors to understand; the deprecation messages will point to the incident issue so the context is reachable. (c) `release.yml` gains one new step (the preflight gate), which adds ~5–10 seconds to every release run. (d) `scripts/` regains two scripts that ADR-106 deleted as "complexity to remove" — they're back as necessary complexity, with stronger framing in ADR-107: they're not "complexity to be eliminated" but "the load-bearing rewrite for npm 9/11 manifest compatibility, gated by a structural safeguard." **What does NOT change:** (a) Public APIs of any package — all five publish at higher versions, same MCP tool surface, same executor behavior. (b) Claude Code plugin distribution — `@ask-llm/plugin` was never affected (marketplace, not npm). (c) The set of supported install paths — `npm install`, `npx -y`, `claude mcp add` all work again for users on the new versions. **Verification methodology** (lessons absorbed): (1) Ran `npm pack` (NOT `yarn pack`) for each MCP after restoring scripts, confirmed tarball shape matches the proven-working 1.6.5 release: 202 files, ~78KB, bundled `node_modules/@ask-llm/shared/`, bundled `node_modules/ask-{gemini,codex,ollama}-mcp/`, manifest `dependencies` field with `*` (not `workspace:*`). (2) Ran the preflight gate locally — all four packages pass; deliberately broke one (removed `prepack` from `gemini-mcp`) to confirm the gate catches it with exit 1 and the right error message. (3) `yarn install` + `yarn build` + `yarn lint` + `yarn test`: all green. **Forward path:** (a) Land this PR via the changesets pipeline; verify post-publish that `npm view ask-llm-mcp@1.6.7 dependencies` is clean (no `workspace:` strings) and `npx -y ask-llm-mcp@latest doctor` works in a fresh shell. (b) Run `npm deprecate` on the four broken versions with a message linking to issue #128 and pointing to the new versions. (c) Open a follow-up Tier-B PR addressing #115 properly: pick between B1 (inline-bundle with esbuild) and B2 (public shared with proper org/scope setup). The choice should be made deliberately with a verification plan that runs `npm pack` (not just `yarn pack`) before merging — the preflight gate enforces this automatically. (d) File the npm 11 global install + `bundledDependencies` bug upstream so other monorepo projects using bundling don't hit the same wall. **Forensic record:** ADR-106 stays in DECISIONS.md; ADR-107 is added at the top. The two ADRs together document the full arc: identify the bug → propose a fix → ship the fix → discover the fix had a verification gap → revert + harden. This is the project's empirical-discipline pattern in action — the cost was real (users were broken for hours) but the institutional memory the incident produced is now structurally encoded in the preflight gate.

**Postscript to ADR-106 (added 2026-05-28):** ADR-106's MECHANISM (remove bundling, publish shared as public) was reverted by ADR-107 after empirical failure. ADR-106's MOTIVATION (fix issue #115's Node 26 global install crash) remains valid and deferred to Tier-B work. The key verification gap that caused the regression: ADR-106 tested via `yarn workspace pack`, but the actual publish path uses `npm publish` (via changesets/action), and the two have different workspace-protocol-rewrite behavior. Future bundling-lifecycle changes MUST be validated through `npm pack` and the `scripts/preflight-no-workspace-protocol.mjs` gate before merge.

## ADR-106: Publish `@ask-llm/shared` as Public — Remove `bundledDependencies` Mechanism (Fixes #115)
- **Date:** 2026-05-27
- **Status:** Accepted (fixes external user bug #115; supersedes the `bundledDependencies` lifecycle established by ADR-052 — preserves ADR-052's motivation of "rewrite `workspace:*` so npm can parse the manifest" but moves the rewriting from a custom prepack script to yarn 4's built-in publish behavior)
- **Context:** External user `twardoch` reported [#115](https://github.com/Lykhoyda/ask-llm/issues/115): `npx ask-llm-mcp doctor` and `npm install -g ask-llm-mcp` crash on Node 26.0.0 with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package '/.../node_modules/zod/index.js' — Did you mean to import "zod/index.cjs"?` from inside `@ask-llm/shared/dist/askResponse.js`. Stack trace lands in Node's `legacyMainResolve`, which is only called when a package's `exports` field is absent. **Reproduced exactly** on Node 26.0.0 + npm 11.12.1 with `npm install -g ask-llm-mcp@0.3.8`. Inspection of the global install layout under `/Users/anton_personal/.nvm/versions/node/v26.0.0/lib/node_modules/ask-llm-mcp/node_modules/` revealed **78 packages with empty placeholder directories** — including `zod`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `express`, `hono`, `jose`, `cors`, `ajv` — every transitive dependency of the bundled `@ask-llm/shared`, `ask-gemini-mcp`, `ask-codex-mcp`, `ask-ollama-mcp` packages that wasn't itself bundled. The bundled workspace packages were populated correctly from the tarball; npm 11 created the directory placeholders for their transitive deps but never actually fetched and extracted them.
